    "content": "Previous Candidates:\n[\"Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (Combined Presentation)\", \"Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (Predominantly Hyperactive/Impulsive Presentation)\"]\n\nInterview Transcript:\n\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm here because I've been having a really hard time with talking too much lately. People around me keep pointing out that I talk excessively, and it's starting to cause some problems in my daily life.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or blurting out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do that all the time. I often find myself blurting out answers before the other person has even finished their sentence. It's just really hard for me to wait my turn in conversations.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: Oh, I'm sorry, I think I missed what you were going to say. Could you please repeat or clarify your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or following through on instructions, and do you often find yourself easily distracted or forgetful in daily activities?\n\nPatient: I definitely struggle a lot with following through on instructions and actually finishing tasks like chores or work duties. As for being forgetful, easily distracted, or paying attention to details, I'm not really sure about those. But once I'm given a task, I just can't seem to complete it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a recent situation where you were working on a task, and if you got distracted, what kind of things usually distract you, such as your phone, other people, or daydreaming?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure what specifically distracts me, and I haven't thought about it much. I just know that I have a really hard time staying on track and actually finishing tasks once I start them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless, fidgety, or have an inability to engage in activities quietly, such as feeling like you're 'on the go' or like you're 'driven by a motor'?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel restless or fidgety at all. I also wouldn't say I feel like I'm \"on the go\" or \"driven by a motor.\" I'm usually able to sit still without feeling that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself making careless mistakes or struggling to stay organized in your daily life, such as with time management, keeping track of belongings, or meeting deadlines?\n\nPatient: I don't really struggle with making careless mistakes or organizing my daily life. I also don't have any issues with losing my belongings or keeping track of them.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by 'talking too much' that you mentioned at the beginning of our conversation, and how it's been causing problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I talk excessively and find it incredibly hard to stop once I get going. I frequently blurt out answers before people can even finish asking their questions and struggle to wait my turn, which really frustrates my friends and coworkers because it feels like I'm constantly interrupting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're doing things on the spur of the moment, without fully thinking them through, such as impulsive decisions or actions, and is this related to your talking too much or interrupting others?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely act on impulse, especially when it comes to how I communicate. I often talk excessively and blurt out answers before people can even finish speaking because I find it so hard to wait my turn. It really feels like I just have to speak immediately without thinking it through first.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me an example of a recent situation where you acted on impulse and did something without fully thinking it through, and how did that turn out for you?\n\nPatient: Just yesterday during a team meeting, my boss started asking a question and I blurted out the answer before he could even finish his sentence. However, the patient also reports difficulty following through on instructions and completing tasks, which is more characteristic of the combined presentation. The lack of reported restlessness, fidgetiness, or feeling 'on the go' does not rule out either diagnosis, as these symptoms are not required for a diagnosis of ADHD. Further evaluation is needed to determine the presence of inattentive symptoms and to distinguish between the two diagnoses.", "is_final": false}
